### 19.2 Direct access, indirect observation and what the census detects (step4-C)

**The finding.** §18.4 item 3 says the channel _exposes no lifecycle value, so a behavior cannot branch on it_. The first half is true. The second is not: the tip's own `applied()` helper in `kernel.browser` mints a channel over a sink that sets a flag, calls it, and reports whether it delivered. Any behavior can do the same in any unit, and R21 does not see it, because it is a call rather than a property read.

**What is decided.** The record distinguishes three things, and D-208's text is qualified in place to say so:

1. **Direct lifecycle access** is a read of the bracket's lifecycle: `#lifecycle`, or an accessor whose body reads it, which at this tree is `closed` and `idle`. It occurs only in `src/kernel/`: the runner, request intake, the runner's ingress refusal, the channel's refusal and unit entries. `BehaviorContext` has no member that returns a lifecycle value. That is what §3 and §18.4 item 3 promise, and it holds.
2. **Indirect observation** is learning whether destruction has been applied from whether an intake acted. **Every intake that refuses after application allows it**, not only `channel`: a behavior whose `dispatch` from a late continuation is refused learns the same fact from its handler not running. So it is a property of having a refusal, and `channel` does not add it. What it can reveal is bounded by §18.3:
   - inside U-C, U-I and U-A it always answers _not applied_, whatever that unit requested;
   - inside U-T it always answers _applied_;
   - outside every unit it tracks application.

   It therefore cannot reveal a request, and its answer cannot change within a unit, so it cannot restore the reading inside a unit that §2.2 withdraws. Library code makes no such probe: the five channels are minted at construction and used only as diagnostic routes.

3. **What the census detects.** R21 (`capture.node`) detects a property read of `#lifecycle` or of a lifecycle accessor, on any receiver, and reports it per member. It does **not** detect:
   - a further read of a listed fact in the same member (step3-B's stated granularity);
   - a destructured alias, such as `const { idle } = this.#bracket`, which the reviewer's mutation AL showed reddens nothing;
   - indirect observation through `channel` or any other refusing intake.

   The channel mint sites are not tested. They are reported by location, by `grep`, in step 6's record beside R21 (§19.4).

**What this changes in D-208's rationale.** The rejection of option (2), a `CLOSED`-only predicate, cited the census: it _cannot tell intake from a reading inside a unit by member_. That remains true, but it does not discriminate between (1) and (2), because the census cannot see a probe either. The grounds that stand are:

- **the surface.** §3 withdraws the lifecycle member. Option (2) would keep a member whose only purpose is to answer a liveness question. Under (1), observing application means using a diagnostic route against its purpose;
- **one refusal.** Under (1) the refusal is written once, in the kernel. Under (2) each behavior writes its own, and every later behavior must remember to.

**This is a non-substantive edit** under the documentation model's §6. It corrects what the decision says about itself, and it changes nothing the decision requires of the code. No `D-*` is minted. §18.4 item 3, §18.5's row (2) and D-208's entry carry dated qualifications. The contract and JSDoc sentences that make the same claim are corrected in step 5 (§19.5).

**Not chosen:**

- **Prevent the probe.** Any refusal can serve as one. Removing the refusal breaks I-36 (a), and refusing while the controller is live breaks R33. A probe made in production code would be visible in the mint-site report.
- **Extend R21 to calls of `channel`.** Every minted channel is called inside units by design, so a call census would flag every diagnostic. The mint sites are the meaningful location, and they are few enough to report.
- **Reopen option (2).** The grounds that stand are sufficient, and reopening it would put back a member §3 withdrew.

### 19.3 The four budget overruns, for the owner's disposition

**Booked figures** are the joint measurement of what landed. The reviewer reproduced the record's _before_ and _after_ columns byte for byte on all 15 rows, and module counts are equal on every row. Brotli governs. Budgets are unchanged.

| Row                       | Brotli before | after |    Δ | budget |    over |
| ------------------------- | ------------: | ----: | ---: | -----: | ------: |
| free drag + bounds        |         8,381 | 8,495 | +114 |  8,423 | **+72** |
| free drag + landing       |         8,400 | 8,437 |  +37 |  8,425 | **+12** |
| free drag complete        |         8,529 | 8,628 |  +99 |  8,576 | **+52** |
| kernel root — `kernel.js` |         6,300 | 6,320 |  +20 |  6,312 |  **+8** |

The sortable-carrying rows shrink by 83 to 148 B. No module enters or leaves any graph.

**What causes the growth.** The reviewer rebuilt the tip with one cause reverted at a time. These are rankings, not a decomposition (CONTRIBUTING §15): Brotli is not additive, so each figure holds only for the row it was measured on.

- **`bounds()`'s copy**, which §2.12 requires, costs +60 B on `free drag + bounds` and +53 B on `free drag complete`. Without it, `free drag complete` would be 1 B under budget and `free drag + bounds` 12 B over.
- **The landing tail's two-field read**, which §2.12 also requires, costs +28 B on `free drag + landing`, the whole of that row's overrun, and +4 B on the kernel root.
- **The channel restructure**, which §18.4 requires, accounts for about 15 B of the kernel root's +20 B. Panic's direct delivery accounts for 1 B. With the landing-tail read reverted, the kernel root is still 4 B over.
- **The two copies that cause three of the four overruns are pinned by no row.** The reviewer's mutation NB, `bounds()` without its copy, reddens nothing. The landing tail's read order is verified only by reading.

**What this acceptance does and does not do:**

- It accepts step 4 with the four rows red. §17.4 states that growth is put to the owner at acceptance and does not stop the step.
- **It authorizes no overrun.** The owner's tolerance of step 2's overruns was step 2's attributed growth (§15.1). It is not a margin for later steps, and it does not cover these.
- No budget is re-based, and no byte-recovery pass is directed. Only the owner can authorize either.
- The owner's priority stands: architecture and runtime ahead of size, with no runtime regression authorized. None is measured.

**The choice, stated for the owner:**

- **Tolerate** the four rows as step 4's attributed size cost, with the rows kept red and nothing re-based, as step 2's were.
- **Re-base** one or more rows, which only the owner can authorize.
- **Direct a recovery pass.** Every byte of the growth comes from a property §2.12 or §18.4 requires. Removing either copy, or the channel, would be a design change and would come back to the architect. A pass would therefore be limited to bytes that do not carry one of those properties.

**The architect's recommendation, which is not a disposition:** tolerate. The growth is the price of properties the design requires, and the owner's priority puts them ahead of size. R34 (§19.4) is required whatever the owner decides, so that the bytes buy a property a row protects.

**Deadline.** The owner's disposition is recorded before `d208-requests.md` is written. Step 6's record states it. Step 5 changes comments and contract text only, so it moves no composition figure and does not wait for it.

### 19.5 Handoff: step 5, the comment pass

**Governing design:**

- §8 step 5;
- D-199's approved comment pass, carried by D-201 ([`snapshot-units-claude.md`](snapshot-units-claude.md) §7) and then by every superseding decision through D-208;
- the documentation model's §5 for source comments and its §6 for what may move into the record.

Sites are cited at `dbbb62199`.

**Scope.** The pass covers the `src/` files that steps 1–4 changed: every library module that differs between D-202's input `6905af249` and `dbbb62199`, with `kernel/kernel.ts` first. The two stories are excluded. After the pass, each comment in those files states what the code there does now, in the present tense.

**The rules, carried unchanged from D-199:**

- **In scope:**
  - counts and relative positions that an unrelated edit can falsify, restated as the property they count or as the sites they name;
  - history narration;
  - passages arguing against code that is not there, shortened to the invariant they protect wherever the code can be read without them;
  - restatements of reasoning already given at the member they cite.
- **Local ordering proofs are kept without exception.** They include the premises of the non-null assertions, the order in which records are dropped, the reason for each `finally`, the order in which arming publishes, and the field order that lets a channel close over a defined sink.
- **There is no target line count.** A passage leaves the source only if the code can be read without it. It goes into the record only if it carries decision reasoning the record does not already hold. Where it does, the record gets it first, in the same change.
- **No runtime statement moves.** Each file is hashed with comments and blank lines stripped, before and after, and the hashes are equal.

**Known stale comments, which the pass must cover:**

- `moveTo`'s published JSDoc says a malformed point _throws at the read_. The read now happens at the call, and the throw at the action's `prepare`. The classification is unchanged;
- `reportError`'s SPI docblock calls it _the one place in the library that invokes a consumer's `onError`_, and says _an implementation catches and discards_. Both composition channels call `merged.onError` themselves, and the throw is absorbed by the channel, not by `#deliver`;
- `BehaviorContext.channel`'s docblock says it _exposes no lifecycle value_. It keeps that and gains §19.2's sentence on indirect observation;
- the README's new bullet says the library invokes no declared consumer slot from application on. It names neither I-36 exception, relinquishment or diagnosis, and it needs to name both.

**Contract text, in step 5:**

- step4-A's passages, with the dispositions §19.4 states, and the census that bounds them;
- step4-C's sentences in I-36's restatement and in 01 §`BehaviorContext`.

None of this is a new requirement, so the contract's normative content does not change. Each amendment is dated and names D-208 step 4 or §19.2.

**Record corrections:** step4-B and the two slips in `d208-step4.md`, as step 5's first commit or within it (§19.4).

**May land in step 5's series:** R34, as its own commit that touches only tests.

**Keep:**

- **The runtime.** Every file's stripped hash is unchanged, so step 5 changes no behavior.
- **Composition.** The build strips comments, so every row is expected byte-equal to step 4's. That is step 5's control row (CONTRIBUTING §18): a difference is a finding.
- **The suite.** The failing set is step 4's: the input's ten rows and the four budget rows.
- No budget is re-based.

**Out of scope:**

- `d208-requests.md`, which is step 6. Creating it would falsify the witness early;
- any change to behavior, surface or tests other than R34;
- the budget overruns, which are the owner's;
- marking D-208, or any F-entry it decides, complete.

Reviewer-E stays the owner's.

**Record:** `.plan/measurements/d208-step5.md`. It holds:

- each file's stripped hash, before and after;
- the composition control;
- step4-A's census, with the class of each hit;
- the corrections made and where;
- R34, if it lands in this step, with its baseline failures and NB's count.

**Escalate rather than decide** if:

- a comment states a property the code does not hold. Report it as a finding. Do not reword the comment to match the code;
- a passage could leave the source only by losing decision reasoning the record does not hold, and writing that reasoning would itself decide something;
- a contract hit cannot be classified as current, history or amended without a decision;
- a passage marked as withdrawn turns out to state a guarantee some code still provides.

**Review.** Step 5 gets its own feature proof before step 6 starts.

**Deadlines for what remains open:**

### 20.2 The census classes, step5-B and step3-B

**Class N is ratified.** §19.4 named three classes and sent everything else to escalation. Step 5's record added a fourth, N: _the matched word is not a lifecycle reference_, such as _a closed union_, _the closed world_, _a live drag_ in a probe, or _participant_ in the tier vocabulary. The reviewer read all 91 N lines, and none refers to the lifecycle.

A hit whose matched word does not refer to the lifecycle cannot state step4-A's property. So it is outside the property, and not a passage the property's three dispositions were written for. N is therefore a classification of the search's false positives, not a fourth disposition. §19.4 is amended in place to say so. This is a non-substantive edit under the documentation model's §6: it states what the census already did, and changes nothing the decision requires.

**02:880 is reclassed from K to E** and joins escalation #2. Its table row 02:872 joins too; the census's terms do not match that row. The same rationale cannot be current in the contract and escalated in the source. §20.3 disposes of them together.

**step3-B is closed.**

- The instrument half was closed at step 4 (§19.4).
- The record half is the dated correction in `d207-step3.md` §Guard-site census, which landed in `ae21d8d1c`. The feature proof verified it against `ef1e4ef6b`, and so did the architect (§20.1).
- From this acceptance, a record or acceptance may cite the ledger census's step-3 table.

**step4-B is closed** with it, including the two `d208-step4.md` slips.

### 20.3 Dispositions

**The classes used below.** They keep factual documentation fixes apart from anything that would decide behavior:

- **F, a factual fix.** A comment or contract line states a property that the code at the tip, or an existing decision, contradicts. The fix states what the code does or what the decision already says. It decides nothing, and it moves no runtime statement.
- **C, a code cleanup with no reachable behavioral change.** It changes runtime statements, but on no path any input reaches.
- **B, a behavioral question.** Answering it would change, or newly ground, a consumer-visible outcome. It needs a decision, and it is not D-208's.

**Why #1 is C and not a decision.** The guard was the downstream half of the `kernel.closed` barrier that step 4 removed from `#seedDraft`. Step 4 removed the barrier and left the guard. Removing it completes step 4's removal, under the same decision. Its required property:

- `admitCommand` has no branch on `#seedDraft`'s result;
- `#seedDraft`'s declared result excludes `null`.

No input reaches the removed branch, so no outcome changes.

**Why #2 splits.**

- **What the code does now.** The source comment and 02:880 say _latched_, and the cancel is now registered. They also give a reason for not closing the gap, _closing it means carrying a per-operation "started" flag_. That reason no longer holds, because the marker exists and the failure arm already consults it. Restating both is F.
- **What it should do.** Closing the gap would suppress the cancel arm's terminal at `MINTED`, as the failure arm does. Keeping it open needs a new ground, because the old one is gone. Either way the answer decides a consumer-visible outcome on a two-fault path.

D-208 does not decide it. Nothing in D-208 was meant to change that outcome, and 5R item 3 establishes it at the tip and escalates if it did. It is raised as **F-442** with its own owner and deadline, and D-208's completion does not wait for it.

**F-442.** It is recorded in `00-index.md` and in the register as O-14.

- **Owner:** the architect decides, and the owner rules if the answer changes a published outcome.
- **Deadline:** before Phase 24 exits. Phase 24 carries no open question.
- **Until then** the gap stays as documented: two faults, one terminal, no start notification (I-31; 02:880).

**Governing design:** §8 step 6. Step 6 starts after 5R is accepted. **Amended 2026-11-03 (§21.8):** it also waits for 5R.1 to close, and the source freeze holds from then until the whole decision is accepted.

- the closed items it relies on, each cited where it closed: R34 (`e46e11493`), step3-B (§20.2), step4-A's census and 5R's supplement.

**Escalate rather than decide** if:

- M-1 or M-2 shows a runtime regression against the input `6905af249` that is attributable to D-208. The owner authorizes none;
- a measurement can only be taken by changing `src/`;
- R21 finds a lifecycle read outside `src/kernel/`.

**Writing `d208-requests.md` is the last act of step 6.** Step 6 gets its own feature proof. The architect's whole-decision acceptance follows it. Only that acceptance does these things:

- removes D-208's unimplemented row;
- closes F-432 to F-435 and F-441;
- marks D-208 implemented.

**Not step 6's:**

- F-442;
- D-200's independent acceptance;
- Reviewer-E.

### 21.1 Assessment

**5R is accepted at `2d17645f5`, with its postcondition qualified.** The recheck finds no implementation defect that blocks it. Three dispositions it routed here are decided below, in §21.2 to §21.4.

**What holds, as the recheck verified it:**

- **Runtime invariance.** Items 1–5 change no runtime statement in any of the 60 `src/` modules, by two tree-based instruments. Item 6's stripped difference is exactly the authorized guard and return type.

- **Item 3** meets them for the path it names.
- **step5-A and step5-C are discharged.** Both controllers' published `requestDestroy` JSDoc names I-36's two exceptions by route, word for word with `README.md:74`, and 01:440 is amended. **The precondition for the whole decision's acceptance that §20.3 set on the published documentation is met.**

**Checked independently by the architect at `ccf08b834`.**

  - `HISTORY_FORMS` (`tests/references.node.test.ts:714–726`) fails every decision, finding or probe number, `§` and `.plan/` path in a `src/` comment.
  - CONTRIBUTING §Comments and JSDoc already forbids `F-*` in source, and allows only one bare _decision_ pointer, in an internal comment.

### 21.2 Item 6's Brotli growth: kept and booked

**Item 6 is kept, and its joint measurement is booked to it** as the recheck reproduced it:

- every row carrying `sortable/spec.ts` falls 9 B minified;
- Brotli moves in both directions, from −17 B to +20 B: `minimal + landing` +20, `both behaviors` +11, `baseline A` +1, and the other four rows fall;
- every free-drag, kernel-root, vocabulary and baseline-B row is byte-equal;
- no module enters or leaves a graph.

No budget is re-based. None of the three rows is near its ceiling: the recheck measured 309, 284 and 347 B of headroom. No row in the owner's pending disposition moves.

**Why it is kept:**

- **The removed code is wrong as well as dead.** `#seedDraft`'s declared `| null` was false after step 4, and the guard branched on a value no path produces. CONTRIBUTING §1.2 prefers the type to the runtime check.
- **The growth is not content.** The minified text only shrinks, and the stripped difference is the authorized removal and nothing else.
- **Reverting would buy the bytes with a falsehood.** Restoring a branch on an impossible `null` to recover a compression artefact is the trade CONTRIBUTING §11 and §13 refuse.

**What §20.4's _any growth is a finding_ did.** It made the change visible, which is a keep property's job (CONTRIBUTING §18). The finding is disposed of here. It is not a regression owed a recovery. The implementer was right to land item 6 and escalate rather than revert. Reverting would have decided the finding.

**For step 6.** 5R's composition row is the figures above, attributed to item 6. Steps 1–5 keep their own.

### 21.3 5r-A: one class of paths, F-442's scope, and the text that claims one path

**The class.** A terminal callback is reached without a start notification exactly when both of these hold:

- a cancel is registered during the activation unit, by consumer code reached from its `prepare` or its `effect`;
- the effect then fails before the start notification.

D-207's entry clause cannot decline the unit, because the cancel arrives after the entry. The registered cancel supersedes the failure checkpoint (D-202 property 7). `#handleCancel` then settles `ACTIVATING` on the phase alone. The outcome:

- `onStart` never runs;
- `onEnd` receives one `canceled` result at `AT_PROPOSAL`;
- the failure reaches `onError` as `drag: failure/superseded-by-cancel`.

**Known instances**, both in the sortable and both probed at the tip:

- **P1**, item 3's: `invalidateInsertion()` throws after the activation record is published.
- **P2**, the recheck's: the placeholder removes itself, and the adjacency check throws before the record is published. The settlement's landing then reads the absent record. It reports `drag: landing/target-unavailable`, whose `cause` is the library's own `TypeError`.

**Not established:**

- a probe of a cancel registered in `prepare`;
- whether free drag has an instance. It shares the kernel arm.

**The outcome predates D-208.** P2 at the program's input `6905af249` has the same terminal and the same `TypeError`. P1's warning code moved from `failure/not-classified` to `failure/superseded-by-cancel`, and that is D-202 property 7's stated change, which D-203 carries. **So D-208 changed no published outcome on either instance.** The hedge §20.3 placed on F-442 is discharged, and D-208's completion does not wait for F-442.

**F-442 is widened to the class, and its tier goes from C to B.**

- **Why B.** On one instance the published diagnostic channel carries a library-internal exception. That is the recheck's tier for 5r-A, and it meets B. It is still not A, because both faults are the consumer's.
- **Owner and deadline are unchanged:** the architect decides, the owner rules if a published outcome changes, and it is due before Phase 24 exits.
- **What the question now covers.** What a cancel applied at `ACTIVATING` does for a drag whose start was never notified, across the class and in both behaviors.

**Any answer to F-442 must meet three properties:**

1. It covers the class, not an instance. It establishes whether free drag and a `prepare`-registered cancel have instances.
2. No instance reports a library-internal exception as a diagnostic's `cause`. On P2 the landing must not read an activation record that was never published. This holds whichever way the gap is decided.
3. A gap kept open states its ground.

**The text is a factual fix (F) and belongs to D-208's remediation.** Four places state that the gap is unique, or bound a claim by its uniqueness:

- `sortable/spec.ts:785–794`, _this is the one path_;
- 02:884, _The one admitted gap_, with its 02:876 row;
- 05:203, _One admitted two-fault gap_;
- `kernel.ts:2029–2034`, whose `ACTIVATING` arm says the start notification has always been delivered.

The adjacency-check passage of §21.1 is a fifth. All five are false about the code at the tip, and each is restated to state the class. That decides nothing.

**The behavioral half is F-442's, and D-208 does not decide it.**

**Step 6's source freeze.** The fix is in `src/`, so it lands before step 6 starts, as remediation 5R.1 (§21.7). It is not absorbed into a measurement record. Step 6 changes no `src/`.

### 21.4 Source citations: the gate's reading is confirmed

The implementer's reading under `d208-step5.md` §Deviations 1 is the only one that complies, and it is confirmed:

- **in source**, the comment states the property, and carries no `F-`, `M-` or `D-` number, `§` or record path;
- **the contract lines** cite F-442;
- **the frame task's figures** are held by `m2.md` §Answer 2.

**§20.4 was wrong to ask for a source citation.** Its item 3 says the text _cites F-442_, and its frame-task bullet says _replace them with a citation_. A finding number in source breaks CONTRIBUTING §Comments and JSDoc by itself, and the package gate is stricter than the repository rule, which is allowed. §20.4 now carries a dated erratum. **No policy changes.**

A section label in a source comment is not a citation of a record, and 5R.1 adds none.

### 21.6 What this acceptance certifies, and what it does not

**It certifies, at `2d17645f5`:**

- **Runtime.** Items 1–5 change no runtime statement. Item 6 removes exactly the unreachable guard and the false `| null`, and changes no reachable outcome.
- **Text.** Every comment and contract line 5R rewrote states what the code does, with three exceptions:
  - the uniqueness text and its twins (§21.3);
  - 5r-B's attribution;
  - 5r-C's wrapping.
- **Published documentation.** Both `requestDestroy` JSDoc blocks name I-36's two exceptions. The published declarations differ only in doc comments.
- **Contract lines.** The four lines step 5 escalated are marked and restated by existing decisions, with their history kept.
- **The census.** The supplementary census classes all 68 lines, with no E.
- **Composition.** As booked in §21.2. The four overrun rows did not move.

**It does not certify:**

- that the admitted gap is unique. It is not;
- F-442's answer, or any behavior on the class;
- whether free drag, or a cancel registered in `prepare`, yields an instance;

## 24. Step 6's escalations disposed, with the owner's budget disposition (D-208)

**Input.** `b3ac91bee` on `drag2/fin-review2`. The record assessed is [`d208-step6.md`](../../measurements/d208-step6.md), over the harness commit `be75b3da1`. The frozen `src/` is tree `4630579d2` at both commits (§23.2).

**The owner's disposition, 2026-11-03, quoted:**

> Rebase the size budgets to the current architecture under the standing budget policy. A compression pass will happen separately, after this phase. This decision does not accept runtime regressions or retained-heap growth.

**What it settles:**

- the four overruns (§19.3). The ceilings re-base, under CONTRIBUTING §18 and the rules `bench/size/measure.ts` states;
- **SC-1.** Its first trigger, _a bundle-budget row goes negative_, has been met since step 4 (2026-11-02), and this is the re-base it schedules.

**What it does not settle:**

- **No runtime regression and no retained-heap growth is authorized.** That answers Escalation 1's question (§24.1).
- **The compression pass is not D-208's.** It follows this phase, and §18 re-bases after its shrink. Nothing here directs a byte-recovery pass.
- **The exact controls.** A control is an equality, not a ceiling, and a re-base of ceilings does not move one (§24.4).

### 24.1 Escalation 1: M-2's retained heap is a regression, and it is recovered in its own unit

**Decided:** the per-controller increase is a retained-heap regression attributable to D-208, and the owner has not accepted it. **D-208 is not accepted while it stands.**

- **The figures** reproduce to the byte at 100 controllers in four rounds: +8 B per sortable controller, +46 to +47 B per mixed pair, +78 to +79 B per free-drag controller. Attribution books about 105 B per controller to `Kernel.channel`'s closures.
- **The design does not require the bytes.** D-208 requires that the refusal is written once in the kernel, and that ~~every diagnostic passes through a minted channel~~ **corrected 2026-11-03 (§25.2): every diagnostic a behavior produces passes through a channel minted from its context, and the kernel's own route applies the same refusal** with the stated semantics (§18.4, D-208 _Ownership and surface_). It does not require three retained closures per controller.
- **Analysis.** The kernel's own mint passes an arrow that wraps `next.reportError` (`kernel.ts:2748`), and each composition mints over an arrow that wraps `merged.onError` (`sortable/behavior.ts:126`, `free-drag/behavior.ts:52`). So there is room to recover within the design. This is not a prescription.
- **Route.** Remediation 6R, a bounded source unit under §21.8, before step 6 resumes (§24.5 step 4). If 6R cannot meet the target without changing a D-208 property, it goes to the owner with the residual figure per population. The owner then authorizes the residual or directs a design change.

**Not decided by this:** the step-4 record's prediction of _one closure per minted channel_. A prediction is not an authorization, and §20.5 already said _the owner authorizes none_.

### 24.2 Escalation 2: M-1's free-drag lean is settled by a pre-registered protocol

**Decided:** the lean is neither dismissed as load noise nor called a regression. The owner accepts no runtime regression, so acceptance needs a positive _no regression_ at a resolution finer than the effect.

- **Why the step-6 rounds cannot settle it:**
  - the lean is one to two timer quanta of 0.195 µs;
  - the two arms sit at p ≈ 0.05 and p ≈ 0.035, two-sided, with four arms looked at;
  - the host's load ran from 6 to 27.
- **The protocol** (§24.5 step 3) fixes the instrument's resolution, the host condition, the primary arms and the decision rule before any round runs.
- **A regression attributable to D-208** is any tip-slower difference against `6905af249` that the rule finds. D-208 carries every step of the chain from that input.

### 24.3 Escalation 3: two baseline defects, F-443 and F-444, neither of them D-208's

The queue's per-sample allocation, and the record's reliance on a heap-growth reading, both predate D-208. Both are equal at `6905af249`. **Neither is a D-208 regression, and neither blocks D-208's acceptance** once D-208's record states I-26 as §24.3.3 does.

#### 24.3.1 F-443: the action queue regrows its store on every sample, and three source comments say it does not

- **Measured** (step 6): about 84 B per pointer sample, booked to `push` under `ExecutionBracket.dispatch`. It is reproduced by `ActionQueue` alone (89–90 B a cycle) and by a truncation to a non-empty length. `clear()`'s `length = 0` releases the backing store, and the next `add` regrows it. The baseline books the same bytes.
- **False comments:**
  - `queue.ts:7–8`: _capacity growth is amortized_;
  - `queue.ts:43–44`: _a drain clears once per pointer sample and truncation allocates nothing_;
  - `execution.ts:78–79`: _the per-action and per-sample paths allocate nothing_;
  - `presentation.ts:375–376`: _the pointer-sample path, which is allocation-free_. The transform string is allocated on that path each sample.
- **Tier C.** No consumer-observable behavior changes, and nothing the repository relies on depends on the comments.
- **The fix is to the code, not only the words.** Runtime wins (CONTRIBUTING §0, §14). The comments state the intended design, and the code misses it. Rewriting the comments to describe a regrowth would document a defect as design.
- **The unit's required properties:**
  - in steady state a pointer sample allocates nothing in the queue's storage. The step-6 harness's calibration row and per-sample arms lose the `push` share;
  - a queued argument still cannot outlive the drain that abandoned it;
  - **no per-controller retained-heap growth at rest** (M-2′'s 100-controller rows), which is the owner's condition;
  - every comment that claims an allocation property states the measured one;
  - if the first three cannot hold together, it goes to the architect.
- **Remeasurement:**
  - the step-6 allocation harness on every arm;
  - M-2′;
  - M-1, paired against the unit's input under §24.5 step 3's protocol;
  - composition against the ceilings and controls in force. `drag.js` and baseline B must not move.
- **When.** After D-208's whole-decision acceptance, because the freeze covers `src/` until then (§23.2) and this is not D-208's. Its own unit. Before Phase 24 exits.
- **D-200.** The file's text is D-200's (`dfdf59a6a`), and D-200's independent acceptance is the owner's. F-443 is information for it, and changes nothing about who decides it.

#### 24.3.2 F-444: a heap-growth reading was cited as bounding per-sample allocation

- **The instrument is unsound for that claim.** M-1's row reads `usedJSHeapSize` growth over 20,000 samples. It returned 0.0 B per sample at both trees on a path the sampling profiler shows allocating about 189 B a sample. Scavenges return the churn before the second reading. It bounds **retention**. It does not bound transient allocation.
- **Tier B.** An instrument the repository relies on is unsound for a claim it was used to make.
- **Decided:**
  - the heap-growth reading is retired as allocation evidence. It stays a retention reading;
  - an allocation claim is stated in measured bytes per unit, with the instrument named. The instrument in force is step 6's sampling heap profiler, with attribution and an arm-minus-control figure;
  - _allocates nothing_ is not written for a path unless that instrument returns zero on it.
- **What it reopens: no acceptance.** Steps 3 and 4 cited the 0.0 B reading as _I-26 holds_ (§17.1 at :1519, §19.1 at :1905). Their conclusion, no per-sample allocation difference attributable to D-208, is re-established by step 6's profiler, which finds every per-sample arm equal between the trees. Only the stated ground was wrong.

#### 24.3.3 I-26, as D-208's record states it

- **I-26's wording and tier stay.** It counts objects created per sample, and it is counted by inspection (05, 06). It is tier C.
- **It is not _allocates nothing_.** One sample allocates about 189 B at both trees: about 105 B is the transform string, which I-26 permits, and about 84 B is the queue's store (F-443).
- **D-208's statement is _no per-sample difference_.** It is measured equal between the tip and `6905af249`, shipped and DEV, with `OPEN_RESOLUTION` per drop and `moveTo`'s copy per call.

**This record's own statements are corrected here,** with the originals left as history. Wherever this record says _one pointer sample allocates nothing_ or _I-26: one sample allocates nothing_, the statement in force is §24.3.3:

- §8 (:544);
- §14.4, property 12 (:1122);
- §15.3 (:1319);
- §17.1 (:1519);
- §17.4 (:1673);
- §19.1 (:1905);
- §20.5 (:2405);
- §23.3 (:2895).

D-201's item 12 in 00-index (_one pointer sample allocates nothing_) is superseded decision text, and it is left as it stood.

### 24.4 Ceilings and controls are administered separately

**Ceilings re-base now, at the frozen tree.** That puts a sensitive instrument in front of 6R, the next source change. Today the sortable-carrying rows carry 284 to 363 B of slack, and `free drag minimal` carries 4 B.

- **The thirteen package rows** re-base to their Brotli figure at tree `4630579d2`, plus the standing 150 B. Five rise: the four overrun rows and `free drag minimal`. The other eight fall.
- **The vocabulary root and baseline B** are administered by their own comments. Both are under their ceilings, and neither ceiling moves.

**Controls stay exact, and are re-declared once, at the end.** That happens at 6R's tree (§24.5 step 5), not at the frozen tree, because 6R is expected to move every kernel-carrying control a second time. Re-declaring after each pass is what `budget-rebases.md` calls a budget wearing an exact number.

- **A control is re-declared only when its whole interval is attributed.** The interval runs from the last figure it held, which was 2026-09-09 for all seven: `free drag minimal` 8,116, `+ bounds` 8,274, `+ landing` 8,276, `complete` 8,426, `kernel.js` 6,185, `drag.js` 142 and baseline B 6,889. It is attributed to named landed changes from the dated records (§18's restoration reading).
- **The six red controls predate D-208.** They were already red at D-204 step 1's input. That includes baseline B, which reads 6,916 against a declared 6,889 and measures a package this one does not build.
- **An interval that cannot be attributed** is a finding for the architect. That control is not re-declared. It keeps its declared figure, and the record states its last reading.
- **No control becomes a ceiling, and no ceiling moves when a control does.**

### 24.5 Handoff: budgets, records and the runtime follow-up

**Owner:** the implementer, except where a step names a reviewer. **Input:** this section's commit. Do the steps in order. A step that escalates stops the sequence there. **A source comment carries no record identifier.**

**The freeze.** `src/` stays tree `4630579d2` through steps 1–3. 6R (step 4) is the one source change authorized under D-208, as its own unit. When 6R closes, the freeze resumes at 6R's tree, recorded by hash, until the whole decision is accepted.

**Step 1 — re-base the ceilings.** One commit, touching `bench/size/measure.ts`, `budget-rebases.md` and `obligations.md` only.

- Set `budget:` on the thirteen package rows to their Brotli figure at `4630579d2`, plus 150 B. Do not move the vocabulary root's or baseline B's ceiling, and do not change any `control:`.
- Write a dated entry in `budget-rebases.md`:
  - measured against `4630579d2`;
  - every row's figure, its old and new ceiling and its slack;
  - the owner's disposition, quoted with its date;
  - the four overruns' causes, cited from §19.3;
  - the sortable rows' fall, cited to the step records that booked it. No new decomposition.
- Add a dated line to SC-1's _Today_: met by its first trigger at step 4, with this re-base as the one it schedules.
- **Keep:** no graph declaration changes. The failing set becomes the six control rows and the four `docs.node` rows, by name. The four budget rows pass.

**Step 2 — correct the allocation claims (F-444).** One commit, `.plan/` only. Make dated corrections and keep the history. Each correction says the heap-growth reading bounds retention only, and states the measured per-sample allocation with its instrument and its two shares. I-26's wording and tier do not change.

- 05:173, I-26's evidence paragraph;
- 05:920, _20,000 samples produced no measurable net heap growth (I-26)_;
- 06:367, _capacity growth is amortized, which is the phrasing every document uses_, which cites F-443;
- `measurements/m1.md` :49, :67 and :97, with a dated note. Measurement records are history, so the figures stay.

Report any other claim of this kind you come across. Do not search for more.

**Step 3 — M-1's follow-up, at the frozen tree against `6905af249`.** Measurement only. Results go in a dated section of `d208-step6.md`.

- **Pre-register first.** Commit the protocol below and its decision rule to `d208-step6.md` before the first round, and cite that commit.
- **Resolution.** The per-sample figure's resolution is 0.05 µs or finer, and the achieved resolution is stated. A harness change goes under `tests/` in its own commit.
- **The host.** Record the 1-minute load average at the start and end of each round. If either reading is above 2.0, discard the round and rerun it. Record the discarded rounds too. If the condition cannot be met, report step 3 blocked on the environment.
- **Rounds.** 24 new paired rounds in ABBA order, not pooled with step 6's.
- **Arms.** The primary arms are free drag bare and free drag `bounds(thunk)`. `axis`, `bounds(element)` and the sortable at 50 and 200 rows are secondary arms.
- **The rule, per primary arm, with ties dropped.** Use an exact one-sided sign test for _tip slower_:
  - **regression:** p < 0.01;
  - **no regression:** p ≥ 0.05, and a median paired difference no larger than the stated resolution;
  - **inconclusive:** anything else.
- **Outcome.** _No regression_ only if both primary arms are. A secondary arm at p < 0.01 is reported as a finding.
- **On regression or inconclusive:**
  1. take a V8 CPU profile of both primary arms at both trees, and report self time by function on the per-sample path;
  2. stop and escalate.

  Attempt no fix.

**Step 4 — remediation 6R: recover M-2's retained heap.** A source unit. Record it in a dated `§Remediation 6R` section of `d208-step6.md`. Its required properties:

- **The target.** M-2′, four rounds ABBA: at 100 controllers, the tip's per-controller retained heap is at or below `6905af249`'s in every round, for all three populations. At 1000 controllers, the tip's range does not lie above the baseline's. **Amended 2026-11-04, on a condition (§26.4):** the four rounds read against this clause stay recorded as read. Whether _in every round_ governs acceptance turns on §26.6's same-tree count.
- **What the recovery comes from.** From what D-208 added: the channel mechanism, its mint sites and their wrappers. An unrelated saving that nets the figure down is reported separately and is not booked as recovery.
- **D-208's properties do not change:**
  - the refusal is written once in the kernel;
  - `channel(deliver)` keeps its signature and semantics, as D-208's _Ownership and surface_ states them;
  - ~~every diagnostic still passes through a minted channel;~~ **corrected 2026-11-03 (§25.2), and superseded for the resumed 6R by §25.5;**
  - R33 passes, and its two mutations still redden the rows D-208's _Order and witness_ names;
  - R21's `capture.node` passes. The mint sites are re-reported by location. Their number may fall, and a rise is a finding. **Qualified 2026-11-03 (§25.3):** the census may be re-pinned once, to the refusal's one new location.
- **The per-sample path is untouched.** No function with a nonzero count in step 6's per-sample call-count window changes.
- **No other cost rises.**
  - Allocation per sample, per drop, per `moveTo` and per committed frame stays at or below step 6's tip ranges. **Amended 2026-11-04 for the resumed candidate (§26.7):** read by a paired rule against the frozen input. The nine cells read above those ranges stay recorded. **Disposed 2026-11-04 for this candidate only (§28.1):** that rule read a3, so this property is not shown to hold. The owner accepted the uncertainty, and it no longer blocks landing.
  - Call counts are identical.
  - The published declarations are byte-identical, or differ only in doc comments.
  - Composition is read against step 1's ceilings. `drag.js` and baseline B are this unit's declared controls and must not move. A row over its ceiling goes to the architect.
- **Escalate rather than decide** if:
  - the target is unreachable within these properties. Report the residual per population and what was tried;
  - the change needs the SPI, a published surface or the per-sample path.
- **Review.** A bounded independent recheck of 6R's diff, against these properties and its measurements. The architect then closes 6R.

**Step 5 — remeasure at 6R's tree, and re-declare the controls.**

- **M-1.** Step 3's result carries over only if 6R's diff touches no function on the per-sample path and the per-sample call counts are identical. Otherwise rerun step 3's protocol at 6R's tree.
- **Composition** at 6R's tree, beside step 1's ceilings.
- **Controls**, in their own commit and their own `budget-rebases.md` entry, under §24.4:
  - attribute each red control's interval from its 2026-09-09 figure to its figure at 6R's tree;
  - re-declare it at the exact landed figure only if the whole interval is attributed;
  - report an unattributed interval and leave that control as it is;
  - `drag.js` stays at 142. No ceiling moves.
- **The failing set** becomes the four `docs.node` rows, plus any unattributed control, by name.

**Step 6 — `d208-requests.md`, the last act.** Write it only when all of these hold:

- steps 1–5 are complete;
- step 3's outcome is _no regression_;
- 6R is closed;
- no escalation or finding from this section is open.

It holds what §20.5 lists, as §21.8 and §23.3 amend it, with these changes:

- I-26 is stated as §24.3.3 states it;
- M-2 is stated at 6R's tree against `6905af249`;
- M-1 is stated under step 3's rule;
- the composition is stated with step 1's re-base and step 5's controls;
- the owner's disposition is quoted with its date;
- the closed items include 6R;
- **added 2026-11-04 (§28.7):** the owner's disposition of that date is quoted with its date. M-2 and allocation at 6R's tree are stated as §28.7 states them.

Then come step 6's feature proof and the whole-decision acceptance, in §20.5's order. **Amended 2026-11-04 (§29.7 to §29.9):** the record's prerequisites and its effect on the witness row are stated there, and the proof runs in the consolidator's round.

**Out of scope:**

- F-442, F-443's fix and the compression pass;
- re-reviewing the comment pass, 5R or 5R.1;
- any source change other than 6R.

### 24.6 What stays open

D-208 stays `Unimplemented (Remediation)`, and the witness holds. F-432 to F-435 and F-441 stay open until the whole decision is accepted. F-442 is unchanged. F-443 and F-444 are raised open here, and neither is D-208's.
