## 25. Remediation 6R's escalation disposed: the kernel's own route applies the refusal and is not required to be minted (D-208)

**2026-11-03. Input:** `4ee87b9ad` on `drag2/fin-review2`, which adds only [`d208-step6.md`](../../measurements/d208-step6.md) §Remediation 6R to the unit's input `0852534bf`. `src/` is tree `4630579d2` at both. The escalation is that section's _Escalation, for the architect and then the owner_, relayed by the owner.

**The working tree.** The relay says the implementer paused with uncommitted 6R changes. The main checkout is clean at `4ee87b9ad`, and the record says every variant was restored with `git checkout src`. No working-tree state is input to the resumed unit. The diffs recorded in §Remediation 6R are.

**Decided, in short:**

- **Within D-208.** The kernel's own route may apply the shared refusal without being minted through `channel()`. D-208 never required that route to be a mint. This record's §24.1 and §24.5 step 4 restated D-208 more broadly than it reads, and they are corrected here (§25.2). D-208 is not amended, and no owner authorization is needed for it.
- **R21 may be re-pinned once,** to the refusal's one new location (§25.3). That admits B′'s form.
- **No residual goes to the owner now.** An in-design candidate exists that has not been measured, and the prototype that reached the target is not that candidate (§25.4). If the candidate misses, 6R escalates with its residual, and the owner decides.
- **6R resumes** under §25.5's properties.

### 25.1 The question

At variant A, the only one that kept every property as §24.5 step 4 wrote them, 6R misses the target by +25 to +26 B per mixed pair and +57 to +58 B per free-drag controller at 100 controllers. With B′ as well, sortable and mixed meet the target, and free drag holds 2,739 to 2,740 B against the baseline's 2,688 to 2,714 B. B′ failed one row of R21's census. Prototype P reached 2,704 B on free drag by giving the kernel's own route an arrow that applies the refusal itself, not a channel minted through `channel()`.

The implementer escalated four questions. Three are decided here: the kernel's route (2), the SPI (3) and R21's pinned location (4). The first, whether to authorize the residual, is the owner's, and it is not put to the owner yet (§25.4).

### 25.2 What governs: D-208's text, and where this record overstated it

**D-208 separates the kernel's own route from the minted channels throughout:**

- _Ownership and surface_ (00-index): _Both behaviors send every diagnostic they produce through a minted channel._ Then: _The refusal is written once in the kernel, for its own route and every minted channel. Panic's bypass stays on the kernel's route._
- §18.4 item 3: the channel _has no panic bypass. Panic's report stays on the kernel's own route._ Item 4 is scoped to **both behaviors**. Item 5: _The kernel's own route and every minted channel apply the same refusal._
- §2 (:95): the refusal _is the kernel's, for the kernel's own route and for every channel a behavior mints through `BehaviorContext.channel`_.
- **At decision time the kernel's route was not a mint.** It was `#notify`, refusing after `CLOSED` with panic's bypass (§18.2). Step 4 implemented it as `this.channel(...)` in `#arm`. That is an implementation D-208 admits, not a property it states.
- **01 §`BehaviorContext`** (01:204) is scoped the same way: _every diagnostic a behavior produces goes through a channel it minted_.

**What D-208 requires of the kernel's route:**

- it applies the one refusal, written once;
- it delivers until destruction is applied and drops every call afterwards;
- it absorbs a throw from the sink;
- panic's report is the only bypass.

It does not require the route to be a channel minted through `channel()`.

**This record overstated it twice.** §24.1 says D-208 requires _that every diagnostic passes through a minted channel_, citing §18.4 and _Ownership and surface_. §24.5 step 4 lists _every diagnostic still passes through a minted channel_ as one of D-208's properties. Neither cited source says that. The restatement merged item 4, which is the behaviors' rule, into the whole. The property list in §24.5 step 4 governed 6R, so the implementer was right to treat P's route as outside it. Both sites are struck and corrected in place, dated, with the originals kept.

**This is a correction of the record to its source, not a reinterpretation of D-208.** D-208's text is unchanged. Its entry in 00-index gains a dated clarification that says so, so that the question cannot be reopened as a reading.

**One contract sentence needs precision.** 05:253, I-36's mechanism paragraph, reads _Every diagnostic the library delivers to `onError` leaves through a channel the kernel minted: the kernel's own route, …_. That is true if _minted_ means that the kernel builds the route per controller, and false if it means minted through `channel()`. A contract sentence should not depend on which. It gets a dated precision in the resumed unit (§25.5). I-36's tier and act (a) do not change.

### 25.3 R21's census: the row is a detector, and it may be re-pinned once

R21's census reads `closed` by enclosing function, and it is an exact-equality list. Its row _should find lifecycle and request reads only at the runner, intake, the channel and unit entries_ names `kernel/kernel.ts › Kernel.channel: closed` because that is where step 4 wrote the refusal. §18.6 states the property R21 holds for D-208:

- the lifecycle is read in `src/kernel/` only;
- no module under `src/sortable/` or `src/free-drag/` reads it;
- `BehaviorContext` has no lifecycle member.

The function's name is the detector's granularity, not part of the property.

**Decided.** The census may name the refusal's new location in place of `Kernel.channel: closed`. These hold:

- **exactly one** census entry is the diagnostic refusal's read of `closed`. It replaces `Kernel.channel: closed` and is not added beside it;
- it is in `kernel/kernel.ts`;
- the entry's comment says what it is: the diagnostic refusal, shared by the kernel's route and every minted channel;
- the row _should find no lifecycle or request read outside the kernel_, the ledger row and every other entry in both lists are unchanged.

A refusal read in two places is two entries. That fails the first condition, and the census then detects a refusal written twice. So the re-pin keeps R21 able to see what _written once_ forbids.

**B′'s form is admitted** under this re-pin: a module function holding the `closed` test and the terminus, bound per mint. It was the census row alone that put B′ outside §24.5.

### 25.4 Why the residual does not go to the owner yet

The owner's disposition accepts no retained-heap growth, and §24.1 routes a residual to the owner only if 6R _cannot meet the target without changing a D-208 property_. That condition is not established.

- **P is not the candidate.** The committed record states that P _writes the refusal a second time_. That breaks _written once_, and R21 was not run on it. Its 2,704 B is the figure a design would have to reach. It is not a reading of a design.
- **The relay describes P as applying the shared refusal.** If a shared-refusal form was measured, its figures are not in the committed record, and they are not evidence here.
- **The candidate is the shared-refusal form.** The kernel's route and every minted channel deliver through one function. The kernel's route is not minted through `channel()`. Its bytes are unmeasured. P is a single run, 9 to 10 B under the baseline's usual 2,713 to 2,714 B on free drag. One A + B′ round read that baseline at 2,688 B, and P would not have met it. A shared refusal may also cost some of the margin. So whether the candidate meets the target in every round is open.
- **The owner's direction of 2026-11-03** (_Stop and escalate … Do not commit source_) chose the escalation path for a question it routed to the architect. It did not decide the reading.

So 6R resumes. If the candidate meets the target, nothing needs authorizing. If it misses, 6R escalates with the residual per population at the best candidate that keeps §25.5's properties, and the owner authorizes it or directs a design change. The architect does not authorize a residual.

**The SPI question (3) is not pursued.** Calling `reportError` detached, or reading it once at arming, changes `BehaviorSpec`'s calling convention. That is an escalation condition in its own right (§24.5 step 4), and the candidate does not need it.

### 25.5 Handoff: remediation 6R, resumed

**Owner:** the implementer, then a bounded independent recheck, then the architect's closure, as §24.5 step 4 states. **Input:** this section's commit. Record it in a dated subsection of `d208-step6.md` §Remediation 6R. **A source comment carries no record identifier.** Everything in §24.5 step 4 holds except where this list replaces it.

**The target** is §24.5 step 4's, unchanged: M-2′, four rounds ABBA against `6905af249`. **Amended 2026-11-04, on a condition (§26.4):** those four rounds were read, and missed the clause in two cells. Protocol M-2″ now decides acceptance.

**What the recovery comes from** is unchanged: the channel mechanism, its mint sites and their wrappers. Removing the kernel's mint site is recovery from what D-208 added.

**D-208's properties, as the resumed unit must keep them** (this replaces the list in §24.5 step 4):

1. **The refusal is written once in the kernel.** One function holds the applied-lifecycle test and the delivery terminus. The kernel's own route and every minted channel deliver through it. No second copy of the test exists. Panic's own terminus is unchanged.
2. **`channel(deliver)`** keeps its signature and semantics, as D-208 _Ownership and surface_ states them.
3. **Every diagnostic a behavior produces** passes through a channel minted from its context. The behaviors' routes do not change.
4. **The kernel's own route** carries every diagnostic the kernel surfaces, to every collaborator that holds it today: the driver, each operation's lifetimes, the controller's unwind guard, and construction's unwind after publication. It applies the refusal in item 1. Panic's report is the one bypass, and it stays as it is.
5. **R33 passes, and three mutations each redden rows.** Each mutation is applied to the shared function or the route and then reverted, and the rows it reddens are reported by name:
   - **M1**, removing the refusal: R33's post-application rows, as D-208 _Order and witness_ names them;
   - **M2**, making the refusal read `REQUESTED`: R33's interval and construction-window rows;
   - **M3**, new: the kernel's route delivering to the sink without the shared refusal. It must redden at least `kernel.browser`'s _should run both scrubs when a reset throws during destroy_, which pins the kernel route's teardown silence. If no row reddens, that is a finding for the architect, and 6R does not close.
6. **R21** passes with at most the one re-pin §25.3 permits. The mint sites are re-reported by location. Their number falls by the kernel's own, and any other change is a finding.

**Unchanged from §24.5 step 4:**

- the per-sample path is untouched;
- no other cost rises: allocation per sample, per drop, per `moveTo` and per committed frame; identical call counts; byte-identical published declarations, or a difference in doc comments only; composition against step 1's ceilings, with `drag.js` and baseline B unmoved;
- the escalation conditions. A change that needs the SPI, a published surface or the per-sample path escalates.

**Text, in the same unit:**

- **Source comments.** The `report` field's documentation and `Kernel.channel`'s documentation in `kernel/kernel.ts` say the controller's own channel is _minted for the armed controller at construction_ and that the refusal is _nowhere else written_. They state what the code does at the candidate. So do the comments in `panic` and the census row that name the channel's refusal.
- **05:253** gets a dated precision, with the original struck. Each behavior's channel, its unwind guard and `FeatureContext.report` are channels minted through `BehaviorContext.channel`. **Corrected 2026-11-04 (§29.2):** that sentence is wrong about the guard and `report`, which report through minted channels and are not minted themselves. The kernel's own route is the kernel's, and it applies the same refusal. The refusal is written once. Panic's report is the one delivery that does not pass through it. Nothing else in I-36 changes.
- Report any other text that states the kernel's route is minted through `channel()`. Do not search for more.

**Measurement**, on the candidate, before anything is committed:

- **M-2′,** four rounds ABBA, every population at 100 and 1000 controllers, with the load recorded;
- **the attribution row,** against the input and the baseline;
- **R33, R21 and the three mutations,** as above;
- **if the target is met,** the costs §Remediation 6R lists under _Not measured_: allocation, call counts, declarations and composition.

**Outcome.**

- **The target is met, with every property above.** Commit the source, the text and the record, and hand the diff to the recheck. Step 5 waits for the architect's closure.
- **The target is missed.** Commit no source. Record the residual per population at the best candidate that keeps every property, and every variant tried, then escalate. The architect forwards it to the owner as §24.1 states. The owner authorizes the residual or directs a design change, and the implementer does not choose between them.
- **A property cannot hold.** Escalate. Do not decide.

**Out of scope:** the SPI's calling convention (§25.4); F-442, F-443 and the compression pass; step 5; `d208-requests.md`.

### 25.6 What stays open

D-208 stays `Unimplemented (Remediation)`, and the witness holds. §24.6's table stands, with these changes:

| Item | Owner | Required action | Deadline |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Remediation 6R, resumed | implementer, then an independent reviewer, then the architect | §25.5 | before step 5 |
| A 6R residual, if the candidate misses | architect, then the owner | the owner authorizes the residual or directs a design change | before step 5 |
| M3 finding, if no row reddens | architect | decide the coverage gap | before 6R closes |

## 26. The resumed 6R's measurement escalation disposed: an unmet criterion, no demonstrated regression, and a pre-registered measurement before any residual (D-208)

**2026-11-04. Input:** `979d51067` on `drag2/fin-review2`, which adds only [`d208-step6.md`](../../measurements/d208-step6.md) §Resumed 2026-11-04 to `04f6348c7`. `src/` is tree `4630579d2`. No candidate source landed. **Checked here:** the diff in §The candidate's whole diff applies to `979d51067` on a scratch index and gives src tree `c0713204a55b24ffc44f9ac0a92d80efa28c7aeb`, as the record states. The other figures below are the record's, not re-run here.

**Decided, in short:**

- **The existing evidence does not establish a residual for the owner to authorize.** It establishes an unmet criterion and leaves growth neither shown nor excluded (§26.1, §26.2).
- **The instrument is investigated first,** under one pre-registered protocol with a fixed end (§26.5 to §26.8). The two missed cells stay on the record as missed.
- **The instrument's premise is false:** heap noise is not one-sided. `drag2:F-445` is raised (§26.3).
- **`in every round` is replaced only on evidence.** If the same tree, measured against itself, misses that clause, a paired rule governs. Otherwise the clause stands (§26.4).
- **No residual is accepted, and no source is committed by this section.** Step 5, the controls and `d208-requests.md` stay closed.

### 26.1 Three different things

| Status | At this input | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Unmet acceptance criterion** | **Yes, twice over.** §24.5 step 4's _in every round_ is missed at 100 controllers: sortable in round 4, mixed in round 2. _At or below step 6's tip ranges_ is missed in nine of the 20 allocation cells | §The rounds, §Costs. This stands, and those rounds cannot close 6R |
| **Demonstrated regression** | **No.** Growth is not shown, and its absence is not shown either | See below |
| **Unresolved measurement uncertainty** | **Yes.** This is what stops a conclusion in either direction | See below |

**Why the two cells do not demonstrate growth** (read from the record's tables):

- The candidate's own figure in the two missed cells is its usual one: 3,626 B and 3,239 B. Only the baseline's figure moved: 3,522 B against 3,701 B, and 3,024 B against 3,276 to 3,277 B.
- The other ten of the twelve cells are at or below the baseline. Free drag's margin is small, at 4 to 7 B.
- The usual figures agree with the arithmetic. The input's 3,709, 3,323 and 2,792 B minus the recovered 84 B give the candidate's 3,625, 3,239 and 2,708 B, which are 76, 38 and 6 B below the baseline's usual figures.

**Why that does not show the target is met:**

- The cause of the low readings is unmeasured. The record says they could be a true low state of the baseline, and nothing here rules that out.
- The further logged rounds came after the governing four, with an added `console.info` inside `retention`. They do not replace the governing rounds, and they are not pooled with anything below.
- A low figure can reach any cell: a low free-drag baseline such as 2,688 B would defeat that cell's margin.

**Why the nine allocation cells do not demonstrate growth:** the candidate's ranges were read alone, against step 6's ranges from another session at another load. No function the diff changes runs in those windows, as far as the code reads. No paired reading or attribution in those windows has established it.

### 26.2 Why +104 B and +215 B are not residuals

A residual, under §24.1 and §25.4, is the per-population growth a candidate carries, for the owner to authorize. These two figures are the candidate's usual reading minus one baseline's low reading. They do not recur in the other three rounds of their population, or in the four logged rounds. Asking the owner to authorize them would put a number in front of the owner that may not describe the candidate. Dropping them would decide the same question the other way, on the owner's behalf. So neither happens.

The owner's condition is unchanged: _This decision does not accept runtime regressions or retained-heap growth._

The implementer's three questions are answered as follows:

1. **Is a round whose baseline figure comes from one low run a miss?** Against the clause as written, yes. As evidence of growth, undetermined.
2. **What reading replaces it?** §26.4 to §26.6, and only on their conditions.
3. **Do the nine cells need a paired reading?** Yes, against the frozen input (§26.7).

### 26.3 The instrument, and `drag2:F-445`

**What is measured.** `tests/perf/m2-prime.browser.test.ts` reads `A − B`, where `B` is taken after the stage is built and `A` after the controllers attach. Each figure is the minimum of five such readings after three warm-ups.

**The premise is false.** Lines 25–26 and 121–122 say heap noise is one-sided, so that _every source of error inflates a reading and none deflates it_. Anything live at `B` and collected by `A` deflates a reading. The same file records one such case: without `settle`, it read 1000 free-drag controllers at −636 kB. §Remediation 6R has a −858 B per-controller reading, and the logged rounds show one of five runs well below the others, at both trees. So the minimum does not filter noise. It can select a deflated reading.

**What the recorded rounds show about the low figures.** A low figure here is more than 2 B per controller below its own set's median, at 100 controllers:

| Set of four rounds | Load | Base, low figures of 12 | Candidate or variant, low figures of 12 |
| --- | --: | --: | --: |
| step 6's rounds | 6.6 – 27.2 | 0 | 0 |
| variant A | 7.27 – 7.90 | 0 | 0 |
| A + B′ | 2.33 – 4.20 | 2 | 0 |
| candidate, governing | 1.88 – 2.66 | 2 | 0 |
| candidate, logged | 2.33 – 3.54 | 1 | 1 |

_Analysis, from those rows._ This table gives three things to test and establishes none of them:

- **The two trees are not separated.** In the three sets at lower load, the baseline has 5 low figures and the tip has 1, of 36 each, and the tips are not one tree. An asymmetry is neither shown nor excluded by so few.
- **Load runs the wrong way for a filter.** Every low figure came from a set at or below 4.20, and none from the eight rounds at 6.6 or above. Those sets differ in day and tree too, so this is no finding about load. It is why M-1's ≤ 2.0 load filter is not adopted here: it could select for the behavior under study.
- **Two candidate mechanisms fit different sizes.** These are hypotheses only.
  - _A previous population still live at `B`._ −858 B per controller at n=1000 is about 4.4 to 4.5 MB below the usual figure, which is the order of a whole population.
  - _Heap housekeeping released between `B` and `A`,_ such as V8's bytecode flushing. The n=100 deficits are 1.7 to 25.3 kB and do not scale with a population.

**The minimum stays the round's figure, with its premise corrected.** It is what every earlier M-2′ figure reports, it reproduces to 1 B when no low reading occurs, and a median of five carries the inflating noise on a 4 to 7 B margin. A low reading can move it in only one round, so the rule across rounds is what bounds that error (§26.6). The median of five is a required second reading that can stop a pass but never make one.

**No earlier acceptance reopens.** Step 6's +8, +46 to +47 and +78 to +79 B are differences between figures that reproduced across all four rounds at load, and §24.1's route stands.

### 26.4 What happens to the target

The owner's condition is the requirement. _In every round_ is this record's way of testing it (§24.5 step 4), so amending that clause is the architect's. The amendment stays conditional, so that it cannot act as a waiver:

- **The four governing rounds stay as read:** unmet in two cells, and stated as such wherever this target's result is stated, including `d208-requests.md` if it is reached.
- **If the same tree misses the clause against itself** (§26.6, count `L` at 1 or more), a miss no longer indicates growth, and the paired rule `R` replaces _in every round_ at 100 controllers.
- **If `L` is 0,** the clause stands, and it is read literally over the protocol's twelve governing rounds.
- **At 1000 controllers,** _the tip's range does not lie above the baseline's_ stands unchanged, and `R` is added to it. This tightens that clause.
- **Allocation:** §26.7's paired rule replaces _at or below step 6's tip ranges_ for this candidate. The nine cells stay recorded as read.

**The owner may overrule this before the first round.** If the literal clause is to stand regardless, the two cells go to the owner now as what they are, an unmet criterion with no demonstrated growth, and the protocol is not run.

### 26.5 Protocol M-2″: instrument, trees and rounds

This section is the pre-registration. **Nothing below changes once a round has run.** If the harness cannot do what this section states, stop and return to the architect.

**Trees.** Each is a scratch worktree on the shared `node_modules`, and the main checkout is not used. Each src tree is checked before the first round and after the last:

| Tree | Revision | Src tree |
| --- | --- | --- |
| base | `6905af249`, set up as step 6's §The baseline | `19f0b867e` |
| input | the harness commit | `4630579d2` |
| candidate | the harness commit, plus §The candidate's whole diff, byte for byte | `c0713204a` |

A candidate edit of any kind voids the rounds taken at it.

**The harness commit** comes after this section's commit and before the first round. It touches only `tests/perf/`, `bench/` and `drag2:F-445`'s dated notes. It holds the rule's computation, and it carries the same measurement code to all three trees, with only the existing `requestDestroy` mapping at base. It provides:

- **Plain mode, the governing instrument.** `retention` is otherwise unchanged: three warm-ups, five readings, `-t per-controller`, ordinary `browser` project, `VITE_DRAG_MEASURE=1`, one file per run.
- **Accounting.** For every reading, including warm-ups: `F` (used heap after clear, settle and `gc()`, before the stage is built), `B` and `A`. They are kept in a numeric buffer allocated before the first reading and printed after the arm ends. Nothing is logged inside a reading.
- **Inject mode.** `attach` also builds a held on-heap array, not a typed array, that adds a nominal 4 B per controller. That is the size of the smallest margin at stake. Nothing in `src/` is involved.
- **Probe D1, a quiesced baseline.** Before `B`, settle and `gc()` repeat until two successive readings agree within 1 kB, for at most three extra cycles.
- **Probe D2.** `--no-flush-bytecode` is added to `--js-flags` for those runs, in the scratch worktree's configuration only. If the engine rejects the flag, D2 is reported as not run, and nothing replaces it.
- **The corrected comments** of `drag2:F-445`.

**Rounds.** One round is one run in each of its two arms, back to back. Rounds are numbered within their set. The first-named arm runs first in odd rounds, and second in even rounds. The blocks run in this order, with each round's load recorded at its start and end. The counts are fixed:

| Block | Set | Arms | Rounds | Runs |
| --: | --- | --- | --: | --: |
| 1 | **G**, governing | candidate, base | 12 | 24 |
| 1 | **AB**, base against itself | base as tip's role, base as base's role | 6 | 12 |
| 1 | **AC**, candidate against itself | candidate as tip's role, candidate as base's role | 6 | 12 |
| 2 | **P**, positive control | base with inject mode, base | 12 | 24 |
| 3 | **D**, probes | D1 and D2, each at base and at candidate, order reversed in even rounds | 12 | 48 |
| 4 | **Alloc**, §26.7 | candidate, input, each in shipped and DEV | 12 | 48 |

Block 1 repeats the fixed cycle G, AB, G, AC, six times, so host drift falls on every set alike.

**What may not happen:**

- **No reading is discarded.** A low or negative reading is a reading. A run is rerun only if it fails a row or does not report every cell and every reading. The rerun keeps its round's number, and the failed output is recorded. More than six such failures across the protocol means it is reported blocked, with the rounds so far and no outcome.
- **No load filter.** §26.3 states why. The load is telemetry.
- **No interim verdict and no early stop.** During the blocks, a run is checked only against the rerun condition above. The rule's computation runs once, after the last round.
- **No further rounds.** Whatever the outcome, more rounds need a new protocol from the architect. The four governing rounds and the four logged rounds are reported beside these and are not pooled with them.

### 26.6 The decision rule for retained heap

**Figures.** `E1` is the minimum of the five readings, the existing figure. `E2` is their median. Both are in bytes per controller, computed from raw bytes.

**The floor and a low figure.** A tree's floor for a population is the median `E1` of its plain-mode figures in Block 1. At 100 controllers, a figure is **low** if it is more than 2 B below that floor. A reading is low on the same test, reading against floor. At 1000 controllers, low means more than 1% below, and it is reported only.

**The paired rule `R`,** for one population and count, over the rounds of one set:

- `d = tip − base`. A **tie** is `|d| < 1` B and is dropped.
- `n` is the number of untied rounds, and `k` is the number with `d > 0`.
- Exact one-sided sign test: `p = Σ_{j=k}^{n} C(n, j) / 2^n`. `m` is the median of the untied differences. If `n = 0`, then `p = 1` and `m = 0`.
- **Regression:** `p < 0.01`. **No regression:** `p ≥ 0.05` and `m ≤ 0`. **Inconclusive:** anything else.

With 12 untied rounds, that is ~~no regression at `k ≤ 9` with `m ≤ 0`, inconclusive at `k = 10`,~~ **corrected 2026-11-04 (§27.4):** no regression only where `m ≤ 0`, which at 12 untied rounds holds up to about `k` = 6, inconclusive above that up to `k = 10`, and regression at `k ≥ 11`.

**`m ≤ 0` is deliberate.** M-1's rule allows `m ≤ r`. This one allows no positive median, because the owner accepts no growth.

**Gates, read first.** If any fails, the outcome is **D** below:

- **V0, continuity.** At 100 controllers, base's floors are within 2 B of 3,701, 3,277 and 2,714 B, and the candidate's are within 2 B of 3,625, 3,239 and 2,708 B. Otherwise the logging changed the instrument, or the tree is not what the record measured.
- **V1, the instrument sees the margin.** In set P at 100 controllers, on `E1`, no population reads **no regression**, and each has `m` from 3 to 5 B. How many read regression is reported. A low figure in the injected arm can flip a round, so this gate asks that known growth cannot pass, which is the property acceptance relies on. The 1000-controller rows are reported, and they are no gate.
- **V2, no typical difference on one tree.** In AB and in AC at 100 controllers, on `E1`, the median of all six differences, ties included, is within 1 B of zero for each population. Six rounds cannot reach `p < 0.01`, so the sign test is not this gate.

**`L`, the clause's behavior with no tree difference.** The number of AB and AC cells at 100 controllers where the tip role's `E1` exceeds the base role's by more than 2 B.

**`T`, the tails.** A two-sided Fisher exact test of low-figure counts, base against candidate, over Block 1 at 100 controllers. Each tree contributes its figures in G and in its own A/A set.

**Outcomes, on `E1` in set G unless stated:**

| Outcome | When | Meaning | What follows |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **A, target met** | V0–V2 pass. At 100, `R` reads no regression for all three populations, and either `L ≥ 1` or G has no round with the tip's whole-byte figure above the base's. At 1000, no range lies above the baseline's and `R` reads no regression. No population reads regression on `E2`. `T`'s p ≥ 0.01 | No growth found by an instrument shown unable to pass a known 4 B. The two earlier cells stay recorded as an unmet literal reading | §26.8 |
| **B, regression demonstrated** | V0–V2 pass, and `R` reads regression for any population, at either count | A residual now exists: `m` and the range of `d`, per population | No source. Architect, then the owner, who authorizes it or directs a design change |
| **C, uncertainty unresolved** | V0–V2 pass, no regression on `E1`, and any of: `R` inconclusive; `E2` reads regression; `L = 0` with a literal miss in G; `T`'s p < 0.01 | Neither growth nor its absence is established | No source and no more rounds. Architect, then the owner, with this label and the figures |
| **D, instrument not decisive** | Any of V0–V2 fails | The instrument cannot answer this as run. The two cells stay unmet and undetermined | No source and no verdict. Architect |

**The cause is reported, and no outcome waits on it.** Each low reading in Block 1 is classed. Its deficit is the floor minus the reading. It is **high-`B`** if `B` is above the median `B` of that run's other four readings by at least half the deficit; **low-`A`** if it is not high-`B` and `A` is below the median `A` by at least half the deficit; and **unclassed** otherwise. Low-`A` does not separate housekeeping from truly lower retention.

**A cause may be called _supported_ only if** all of these hold at the tree in question:

- at least 80% of its low readings fall in one class;
- the matching probe (D1 for high-`B`; D2 for low-`A`, and for its one mechanism only) shows no low figure in its 36 at 100 controllers;
- Block 1's rate at that tree predicts at least three low figures in 36;
- the floors under that probe stay within 2 B.

Otherwise the cause is **unestablished**, a permitted result. It is carried as a named limit into 6R's closure and `d208-requests.md`.

**What rule `R` does not settle.** `R` compares a tree's usual state. If a true low state exists at either tree and stays unexplained, it is outside what `R` measures, and the record says so.

### 26.7 Paired allocation against the frozen input

**Instrument.** `tests/perf/d208-step6.browser.test.ts`, as in step 6, in Block 4. It is paired against the **input**, because step 6's tip ranges are that tree's. The baseline does not enter.

**Rule, per cell.** The rule's form is `R`'s, with these changes:

- `d = candidate − input`, in bytes per unit;
- a tie is `|d| < 0.05` B, the same figure at the reported precision;
- **regression:** `p < 0.0005`. **No regression:** `p ≥ 0.0025` and `m ≤ r`. **Inconclusive:** anything else.

**Clarified 2026-11-04 (§27.1):** `m` and `r` compare unrounded, as the computation committed before the first round does.

The α values are M-1's 0.01 and 0.05, each divided by the 20 cells, so the family's false-alarm rate stays near those levels. With 12 untied rounds, only `k = 12` is regression. `r` is the width of that cell's tip range in step 6's tables, which is the spread the old clause tolerated at one tree:

| Cell                         | `r`, shipped | `r`, DEV |
| ---------------------------- | -----------: | -------: |
| sortable sample              |          1.0 |      0.9 |
| free drag, bare              |          3.6 |      0.8 |
| free drag, `bounds(element)` |          4.6 |      5.4 |
| free drag, `bounds(thunk)`   |          0.9 |      0.2 |
| free drag, `onMove`          |          0.3 |      0.2 |
| committed frame, moving      |           62 |       59 |
| committed frame, still       |           10 |        9 |
| free-drag drop               |           42 |       15 |
| sortable drop                |           34 |       39 |
| `moveTo`                     |          7.7 |      1.0 |

**Attribution, in the same runs.** At the candidate, in each of the 20 windows, the sampler books no bytes to a function the diff changes: `ArmedController`'s constructor, `Kernel.channel`, `Kernel.#arm`, `deliverUnlessApplied` or `reportToSpec`. Any such byte is a finding.

**Outcomes:**

- **a1, no other cost rises:** every cell reads no regression, and attribution is clean.
- **a2, regression demonstrated:** any cell reads regression. No source. Architect, then the owner, with `m` per cell.
- **a3, uncertainty unresolved:** any cell is inconclusive, or attribution finds a byte, with no cell reading regression. No source. Architect.

### 26.8 Handoff

**Owner:** the implementer. **Input:** this section's commit.

1. Build the harness commit that §26.5 states. Commit it before the first round.
2. Run Blocks 1 to 4 under §26.5.
3. Compute the outcome once, then record it in a dated subsection of `d208-step6.md` §Remediation 6R. It holds the harness commit, every round's figures and load, the gates, `L`, `T`, each `R` and each allocation cell, the low-reading classes and the cause's status. It states each result under one of §26.1's three labels. **Every reading goes in a raw data file committed under `.plan/measurements/`.** The earlier subsections are not edited.
4. **Act on the outcome:**
   - **A and a1:** apply the candidate's diff byte for byte. Commit the source, the text and the record as §25.5 states, and hand the diff to the bounded recheck. The recheck also checks this protocol's record against its raw file. The architect then closes 6R. The two cells stay stated as missed under the literal clause.
   - **Anything else:** commit no source, and escalate with the outcome's label and figures. **Disposed 2026-11-04 (§28):** this branch ran, and the escalation reached the owner. The owner's disposition lets this candidate land on A and a3. No other outcome or candidate is covered.

**Carried, and not re-measured,** unless the candidate's diff changes: R33, R21 with its one re-pin, M1 to M3, the call counts, the declarations, composition and the attribution row. They remain the record's readings, and the recheck tests them.

**Out of scope:** any source change other than applying that diff under outcome A and a1; another candidate; replacing the estimator beyond this protocol; step 5 and control re-declaration; `d208-requests.md`; F-442 and F-443.

### 26.9 Limits of this disposition

- **Nothing here measured anything.** The only check run was the diff's application on a scratch index. §26.3's table is computed from the record's published figures. The record commits only three rows of the logged five-reading data, so nothing here rests on the rest of it.
- **The rule's thresholds were chosen after seeing these rounds,** though before any round they will judge. V1 and V2 test them, and the literal reading stays beside them. So they are not independent of the two missed cells.
- **The mechanisms in §26.3 are hypotheses.** Bytecode flushing as a cause, and the flag's effect in this Chrome build, are unverified.
- **At 1000 controllers,** a 4 B difference is likely below the spread. That row bounds gross growth only.
- **Chromium only,** one machine, as before.
- **The run count is about 168 runs.** No per-run time has been measured for `-t per-controller`. M-1's follow-up ran 72 runs in about 2.4 hours, which gives an order of magnitude only.

### 26.10 What stays open

D-208 stays `Unimplemented (Remediation)`, and the witness holds. §25.6's table stands, with these changes:

| Item | Owner | Required action | Deadline |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Protocol M-2″ and the paired allocation | implementer | §26.8 | before 6R closes |
| 6R's two literal cells and nine allocation cells | architect | state their result under §26.6 and §26.7's outcome | at 6R's closure |
| A residual, only under outcome B or a2 | architect, then the owner | the owner authorizes it or directs a design change | before step 5 |
| Outcome C, D or a3 | architect, then the owner where an authorization is needed | decide. No more rounds without a new protocol | before step 5 |
| `drag2:F-445`, the one-sided premise (tier B) | implementer for the corrections; architect for any later estimator | §26.8; then decide after the protocol's result | corrections in the harness commit; estimator before Phase 24 exits |

## 27. Protocol M-2″'s outcome disposed: A and a3 stand as read, and one final bounded question comes before the owner is asked (D-208)

**2026-11-04. Input:** `8451f1fe6` on `drag2/fin-review2`, over the harness commit `550777c9c`. `src/` is tree `4630579d2`, and no candidate source landed. The result is [`d208-step6.md`](../../measurements/d208-step6.md) §Protocol M-2″, with [`d208-m2pp-raw.jsonl`](../../measurements/d208-m2pp-raw.jsonl) and [`d208-m2pp-rule.json`](../../measurements/d208-m2pp-rule.json).

**Decided, in short:**

- **A and a3 stand as the historical result.** Rounding `moveTo`'s figures after the result would change acceptance, and it is not adopted.
- **No regression is demonstrated, and none is excluded.** `moveTo`'s lean is not a 0.01 B technicality. It has the same sign count in both projects, and a post-hoc test finds a lean across the allocation windows (§27.2).
- **The anonymous-frame limit does not touch the inconclusive cell.** It affects the four drop cells, and a call-count check closes it (§27.3).
- **The owner is not asked yet.** One measurement can answer what the owner would otherwise be asked to accept blind, so the architect issues protocol Q-a3 under §26.10 (§27.5 to §27.7).
- **Q-a3 is the last measurement on a3.** If it does not discharge a3, an explicit disposition goes to the owner, and no further rounds run.

**Overruled by the owner 2026-11-04, as §27.7 provides, before any round (§28).** Q-a3 is not run, and §27.5 to §27.8 stay only as the text of a protocol that never ran. The rest of this section stands.

### 27.1 The precision question

**The computation committed before the first round governs.** §26.5 put the rule's computation in the harness commit, and §26.5 says nothing changes once a round has run. That computation compares unrounded figures: `m` 1.010 B against `r` 1.0 B. So `moveTo` in DEV reads inconclusive, and the outcome is a3.

§26.7 can be read either way. It states `r` to 0.1 B and calls a tie _the same figure at the reported precision_. The ambiguity is this record's. But the implementer's rounded reading was computed after the outcome was known, and it would turn a3 into a1 with no new evidence. It is correctly reported and correctly not applied, and §26.7 is clarified below for any later use.

**A 0.01 B margin would also be the wrong thing to settle,** for three reasons read from the committed data:

- **`r` is not a physical tolerance.** It is one cell's spread in four earlier rounds: 1.0 B in DEV and 7.7 B in shipped, on the same path. The shipped width comes from one low reading, 343.8 B, and that kind of reading recurs at the input in this block: 343.64 B in round 9. Shipped `moveTo` passes on that wider `r`, with the same `k` of 9 of 11 and `p` of 0.0327.
- **The same tree moved more than `r` between sessions.** The input reads 349.2 to 350.6 B in DEV here, against step 6's 350.4 to 351.4 B.
- **Either side of the line, the evidence is the same.** A result that flips on 20 B in a 700 kB window is not a finding about the library.

### 27.2 What the uncertainty is

_Everything in this subsection is analysis of the committed raw file, done here after the outcome was known. It does not change the outcome._

**Checked independently of the implementer's scripts:** set G's six rows and `L`, recomputed from the raw readings, match the record, as do the two `moveTo` rows.

**Against reading the lean as noise:**

- **Both projects lean the same way:** `k` 9 of 11 in each, with `m` 1.01 and 0.61 B. The two projects' differences correlate at only 0.37 across rounds.
- **Other cells lean too.** Five of the 20 cells have one-sided `p` under 0.05, where about one would be expected. They are `moveTo` in both projects, `onMove` shipped, and DEV's sortable sample and moving frame.
- **A family test finds a lean.** Let `S` be the sum over cells of `k − n/2`. Flipping each round's two arms, which keeps the correlation between a run's cells, gives `S` 20.5 and an exact `p` of about 0.002 across both projects. With `moveTo` left out, `S` is 13.5 and `p` is about 0.023. This test was chosen after seeing the data, so it only raises the question. It answers nothing.

**Against reading the lean as a per-call cost of the diff:**

- **It is too small to be one more object per call.** 0.6 to 1 B per call is less than any object costs, so a per-call object is out. A rare allocation, or a once-per-window lump of about 1 to 5 kB, would fit. These windows are 2,000 calls long, and they cannot separate those from each other.
- **No one frame carries it.** Among the six library frames that carry the `moveTo` window, the largest paired median is about 0.5 B, the largest `k` is 8 of 10, and no frame carries the total in both projects. Bytes also swap between library and _other_ by about ±4 B in single rounds, which suggests attribution shifting rather than new work.
- **The changed functions book nothing,** in all 20 windows and 24 candidate runs, and as far as the code reads, none of them runs in those windows.
- **The ranges overlap:** candidate 349.2 to 351.7 B and input 349.2 to 350.6 B in DEV.

**A possible confound was never tested.** The arms differ in code and also in worktree. A lean across windows that do not run the changed code could come from the tree's identity rather than its code. Nothing in M-2″ separates the two.

**So, under §26.1's labels:**

| Label | State |
| --- | --- |
| **Unmet criterion** | Yes. a1 is not reached, and 6R cannot land on this result |
| **Demonstrated regression** | No. No cell reaches `p < 0.0005`, and the family test was chosen after the fact |
| **Unresolved uncertainty** | Yes: whether the candidate costs a small amount more per `moveTo`, and whether the lean across windows follows the code. The upper size is about 1 B in 350 per call on the median |

**The nine earlier cells are not the question.** The nine cells that read above step 6's ranges in the resumed 6R all read no regression here, and the a3 cell is not one of them. Those readings stand and are not re-judged.

### 27.3 The anonymous-frame limit

**What it covers.** `kernel/kernel.ts:(anonymous)` books bytes in the two drop rows only. In the raw file it books nothing in `moveTo`, in the sample windows or in the committed-frame windows. So it cannot bear on the a3 cell.

**What it is.** In those rows the frame reads about 2 to 20 B per drop, or about 217 to 276 B in some rounds, at both trees. In the free drop, that is four of twelve rounds at the input, shipped, and two of twelve at the candidate, DEV. A similar step of about 250 B, or 12.5 kB in a window, also appears on `kernel/pointer.ts:(anonymous)`, `kernel/presentation.ts:(anonymous)`, `kernel/unwind.ts:(anonymous)` and `sortable/spec.ts:(anonymous)`, at both trees. So one lump lands on whichever anonymous frame is active. The input has this frame without the candidate's arrow. This does not prove the arrow books nothing.

**The record's wording needs one correction.** It says _the profile cannot name which anonymous function_. The harness's key is what loses it. `attribute` keys on `functionName` alone, and its `CallFrame` type reads only two fields, while CDP's call frame also carries a line and column. That is read from the harness and the protocol's definition, and not tried here.

**My §26.7 list omitted the arrow, so this gap is mine.** It listed the changed functions by name, and the diff's arrow has none. The implementer was right not to count it as a finding.

**What closes it.** A function that is not called allocates nothing. §27.5's Q1 asks whether it is called, by exact count. That is a more direct answer than attributing bytes to it.

### 27.4 Retained heap

**Outcome A stands as read, on the rule pre-registered for it.** Five literal misses are on the record: two in the resumed 6R and three in set G, at 100 controllers. Each is the candidate's usual figure against a low base figure. `L` is 2, so the same tree missed the clause against itself, and §26.4 hands the clause to `R`.

**Evidence beside the rule, which it does not rest on.** The floors' differences hold under both probes: 74.6, 38.3 and 5.9 B under D1, and 75.6, 39.0 and 5.9 B under D2. The arithmetic of §26.1 gives 76, 38 and 6 B.

**The limits it carries to closure:**

- **The low state's cause is unestablished,** and it is outside what `R` measures.
- **Low figures at 1000 controllers lean to the candidate:** 21 of 72, against 5 of 72 at base. `T` covers only 100 controllers, by its terms, and lower candidate minima could flatter `E1` at 1000. `E2`, which a low reading cannot set alone, reads the same way, with `k` at most 2 of 12 and `m` −58.1, −31.5 and −5.4 B. So this does not change the outcome, and it stays stated.
- **At 1000 the row bounds gross growth only.** V1's 1000-controller rows read inconclusive at the 4 B injection.

**A correction to §26.6's gloss.** It said _no regression at `k ≤ 9` with `m ≤ 0`_. As the implementer found, 12 untied differences with at least 7 positive have a positive median, so that reading holds only up to about `k` = 6. No retained row is near it: G's largest `k` is 3. The rule's text is unchanged, and the gloss is corrected in place.

### 27.5 Protocol Q-a3: the questions

**Withdrawn 2026-11-04, unrun (§28.2).**

This section is the pre-registration. **Nothing below changes once a round has run.** If the harness cannot do what this states, stop and return to the architect. M-2″ is not repeated. Retained heap is not measured, and no outcome from it is reopened.

**Q1, deterministic: does the candidate run anything differently in these windows?** In each of the ten allocation windows, in each project, is the list of `src/` functions with a nonzero call count, and each one's count, identical at the candidate and the input? This includes anonymous functions, so an added or lost call to the candidate's arrow changes the list.

**Q2, per call: does `moveTo` cost more at the candidate, once a lump cannot pass for a per-call cost?** At 20,000 calls in a window, is the candidate's per-call figure above the input's by more than 0.25 B, in either project? A per-call cost of 0.6 to 1 B stays that size at 20,000 calls, and a fixed lump of the size seen falls to about 0.1 B. 0.25 B lies between them. It is also tighter than the `r` values that governed a1 for this cell, so passing is not a relaxation.

**Q3, the lean: does it recur on fresh rounds, and does it follow the candidate's code?** Does `S` lean at the candidate on fresh rounds? Does it also lean at a tree whose `src/` is byte-identical to the input, in another worktree?

### 27.6 Protocol Q-a3: instrument and rounds

**Trees,** each checked before the first round and after the last:

| Tree | Revision | Src tree |
| --- | --- | --- |
| input | the harness commit | `4630579d2` |
| candidate | the harness commit, plus §The candidate's whole diff, byte for byte | `c0713204a` |
| placebo | the harness commit, in a worktree whose directory name has the same length as the candidate's | `4630579d2` |

**The harness commit** comes after this section's commit and before the first round, and it touches only `tests/perf/` and `bench/`. It holds the rule's computation. It adds, in `tests/perf/d208-step6.browser.test.ts`:

- **The scaled window.** Directly after the existing `moveTo` window, the same fixture runs a 20,000-call window. Its points are built before the window opens.
- **The known window.** Directly after that, a further 20,000-call window also allocates a small short-lived array about every 24th call, a nominal 1 B per call. The harness counts it. Its exact size is stated in the harness commit.
- **A count mode.** In it, each of the ten windows runs under the existing precise coverage instead of the sampler. Every `src/` function with a nonzero count is reported by file, name and count. Anonymous functions are not merged into one key.

No allocation window before these two changes. Count mode runs apart, because coverage changes what the engine optimizes.

**Rounds.** The counts are fixed:

| Block | Set | Arms | Rounds | Runs |
| --: | --- | --- | --: | --: |
| 1 | **Counts** | candidate, input, count mode, each in shipped and DEV | 2 | 8 |
| 2 | **C** | candidate, input, each in shipped and DEV | 12 | 48 |
| 2 | **Pl** | placebo, input, each in shipped and DEV | 12 | 48 |

Block 2 alternates C and Pl, one round each. The first-named arm runs first in odd rounds of its set, and second in even rounds. That is 104 runs. M-2″'s allocation block ran 48 such runs in about 9 minutes.

**The rules of §26.5 apply:** no reading is discarded, no load filter, no interim verdict, no early stop, and a run is rerun only if it fails a row or does not report every cell, with the failed output recorded. More than four such failures means it is reported blocked, with no outcome. The computation runs once, after the last round. M-2″'s rounds are reported beside these and are not pooled with them.

### 27.7 Protocol Q-a3: the rule and its stopping condition

**Q1.** In each window and project, the two trees' lists are compared. A count that varies between rounds of one tree is reported, and it is not a difference between trees. **Clean** means no other difference. Any other difference is named by function, and it means Q1 is **not clean**.

**Q2,** per project, in set C on the scaled window, with `d = candidate − input` in bytes per call, unrounded, and `|d| < 0.005` a tie. `k`, `n`, `p` and `m` are as in `R`.

- **Per-call growth excluded:** `m ≤ 0.25`.
- **Per-call growth demonstrated:** `m > 0.25` and `p < 0.005`, the 0.01 of `R` divided by two projects.
- **Inconclusive:** anything else.

The 2,000-call window's cell is computed the same way, and it is reported only.

**Q3,** per set, across all 20 cells of §26.7, excluding the two new windows. `S` is the sum over cells of `k − n/2`, with §26.7's tie. Its exact `p` comes from flipping a round's two arms, per project, and combining the two projects' distributions.

- **Lean:** `p < 0.01`. **No lean:** `p ≥ 0.05`. **Unsettled:** anything else.

A cell reading `p < 0.0005` in set C is a regression finding. No other cell verdict from these rounds counts, because M-2″'s Block 4 is the reading of those cells. That keeps this from being a second draw at them.

**Gates, read first:**

- **K, the instrument sees the size at issue.** The known window minus the scaled window, within each run, has a median of at least 0.5 B per call over Block 2, in each project.
- **Z, the worktree alone does not produce the size at issue.** In set Pl, on the scaled window, `m ≤ 0.25` in both projects.

**Outcomes. There are exactly three, and this is the stopping condition:**

| Outcome | When | §26.1 label | What follows |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **discharged** | K and Z pass. Q1 is clean. Q2 reads excluded in both projects. No cell reads `p < 0.0005`. Q3 reads no lean in C, or lean in C and lean in Pl with Pl's `S` at least half of C's | **Uncertainty resolved for this path.** a3 stays the historical result, and the bound stated is `m` | §27.8, item 1 |
| **regression** | K and Z pass, and Q1 is clean, with any of: Q2 reads demonstrated in either project; a cell reads `p < 0.0005`; Q3 reads lean in C and no lean in Pl | **Regression demonstrated,** with figures per project and per cell | §27.8, item 2 |
| **uncertain** | Anything else, including K or Z failing, Q1 not clean, or Q2 or Q3 unsettled | **Uncertainty unresolved** | §27.8, item 2 |

**Why `discharged` is a legitimate change and rounding is not.** Both alter how a3 could be left behind. Rounding would alter the reading of existing figures after the outcome, with no new evidence. `discharged` is fixed here, before its rounds, and needs new evidence on a tighter bound. It still follows a failed cell, so the selection is disclosed, and the closure states it.

**The owner may overrule this before the first round.** If a3 is to go to the owner now, the disposition in §27.8's item 2 is sent with M-2″'s figures, and Q-a3 is not run.

### 27.8 Handoff

**Replaced 2026-11-04, unexecuted, by §28.4 to §28.7.**

**Owner:** the implementer. **Input:** this section's commit.

1. Build the harness commit that §27.6 states, and commit it before the first round. Run Blocks 1 and 2. Compute the outcome once, then record it in a dated subsection of `d208-step6.md` §Remediation 6R, with every reading in a raw data file under `.plan/measurements/`. **On `discharged`:** apply the candidate's diff byte for byte, then commit the source, text and record as §25.5 states, and hand the diff to the bounded recheck. The recheck also checks M-2″'s and Q-a3's records against their raw files. The architect then closes 6R, stating A, a3, `discharged`, the five literal cells and §27.4's limits.
2. **On `regression` or `uncertain`:** commit no source, and stop. The architect forwards an explicit disposition to the owner, carrying the outcome's label, its figures and the architect's recommendation. The owner chooses one: authorize the stated bound, direct a design change, or commission a further named question. **Measurement on a3 ends here unless the owner commissions more.**

**Carried, and not re-measured,** unless the candidate's diff changes: M-2″'s outcome A, and §26.8's carried list.

**Out of scope:** another candidate; retained-heap measurement; the low state's cause; replacing the estimator; step 5 and control re-declaration; `d208-requests.md`; F-442 and F-443.

### 27.9 Limits of this disposition

- **No rounds ran here, and no source changed.** The analysis in §27.2 and §27.3 is computed from the committed raw file after the outcome was known. It gives Q-a3 its questions, and it decides nothing.
- **The 0.25 B bound does not show zero.** Under `discharged`, a sub-bound difference may remain, and it is stated as a bound.
- **The placebo tests a worktree's identity only.** Other differences between the two checkouts, such as the candidate's text in comments, stay untested, and `discharged`'s lean clause cannot separate them.
- **That CDP frames carry a line and column,** and that a call-count zero rules out allocation in that function, are read from the protocol and reasoned, not run here. Q1 tests the second.
- **Chromium only,** one machine, as before.
- **§26.9's time estimate was wrong.** It offered hours by analogy with M-1's follow-up. M-2″ took about 18 minutes for 168 runs. That is part of why another bounded measurement comes before asking the owner.

D-208 stays `Unimplemented (Remediation)`, and the witness holds. §26.10's table stands, with these changes:

| Item | Owner | Required action | Deadline |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| `drag2:F-445`, the estimator | architect | M-2″ left the low state's cause unestablished, and the estimator decision stays open | before Phase 24 exits |

### 28.5 Handoff 2: the bounded recheck

**Owner:** an independent reviewer, in a fresh session. **Input:** the landing commit. **Artifact:** `.plan/reviews/fin-review2/d208-6r-recheck-claude.md`. The reviewer changes no source and closes nothing.

**Reading and existing rows only.** Everything below is deterministic, so running it is not a statistical round.

**Identity.** The landed objects equal §28.3, and the landing commit touches only those five files and its record.

**The refusal.**

- `closed` is tested once for diagnostics, in `deliverUnlessApplied`. The kernel's route and every minted channel deliver through it.
- Panic stays the one bypass, behind its own terminus.

**`channel(deliver)`.**

- Its signature is unchanged. `deliver` still receives `undefined` as its receiver, and an extra argument is still ignored.
- A throw from `deliver` is absorbed. A channel minted in the factory delivers before arming, and every channel drops after application.
- The bound bracket is the object the replaced arrow read at each call.

**The kernel's route.**

- Every collaborator that held it still holds it: the driver, each operation's lifetimes, the unwind guard, and construction's unwind after publication.
- `reportError` is still read and called with its receiver at each delivery, after the refusal. `this.#spec` is the object the replaced arrow called as `next`.
- **New in this diff: the route is now built inside the constructor, before `#bracket` and `#spec` are assigned.** The test of `closed` sits outside the `try`, so a report before those assignments would throw instead of being absorbed. Confirm by reading that no path reaches the route that early.

**Diagnostic outcomes.** D-208's _required behavior_ list holds, each item pinned to a row:

- teardown is silent;
- a panic delivers one `DraggableError` with stage `null`;
- a late `FeatureContext.report` is dropped, and an earlier one is delivered;
- the pre-arm composition unwind is delivered;
- a throwing hook at retirement on a live controller is reported.

**R33 and R21.**

- R33 passes in full.
- `capture.node` passes with exactly the one re-pin §25.3 permits, and no other census entry changes.
- The mint sites are the four the record names.
- No module under `sortable/` or `free-drag/` reads the lifecycle.

**The mutations.** Apply M1, M2 and M3 as §25.5 states, one at a time, and restore after each. Report the rows each reddens by name, against the record's 19, 11 and 2. A difference is a finding.

**Text.**

- 05:253's precision and the changed comments state what the code does. No source comment carries a record identifier.
- The published declarations differ only in `BehaviorSpec.reportError`'s doc comment.

**The premises the acceptance rests on.**

- **By reading:** no changed function, and not the anonymous arrow, runs in the allocation windows unless a diagnostic is reported. §27.2 and §27.3 lean on this. If it is false, that is a finding, even without a new measurement.
- **Run:** the existing call-count row once in each project, against §Costs. §24.5 step 5's M-1 carry depends on it.

**Out of scope:**

- the low readings' cause, the estimator and F-445;

**Findings.** A correctness or semantic defect, a failed premise or a record mismatch is reported with its evidence and goes to the architect. Reporting one does not depend on the size of its allocation effect. An allocation figure that merely reads differently is not a finding, and no round is run to produce one.

The contract offers none of these uses, and repository policy is not to defend against invalid use. No row is owed. ### 29.2 reviewer-2: corrected here

**The finding is right, and the error is this record's.** §25.5 prescribed the sentence, and the implementer followed it. There are four mint sites: each behavior mints its own channel and the composition's. The unwind guard is `createUnwind(this.#notify)`, a function reporting through the behavior's own channel. `FeatureContext.report` is a closure that builds a warning and calls the composition's channel. All of this is read at `sortable/spec.ts:317–318`, `sortable/behavior.ts:126` and `:146`, and their free-drag twins.

**What holds, and what was wrong.** Every diagnostic on those routes does pass through a minted channel, so I-36's mechanism and tier are unaffected. Only the statement of what is minted was wrong.

**Done in this unit:** 05:253 carries a dated correction, with the prescribed wording struck, and §25.5's bullet carries a pointer. D-208's _Ownership and surface_ already says _send … through a minted channel_, which is true, and it does not change.

**§28.3's rule is extended.** The reviewer asks because `05`'s blob is one of §28.3's objects. That row checked the landing. M-2″ measured `src/`, and contract text does not run, so correcting it does not touch the acceptance.

### 29.3 reviewer-1: corrected at the reported sites, and nowhere else

**The finding is right.** The comments state a form the code no longer has. They are tier C, and they do not block closure. They are still corrected before the final figures and the final proof, because §25.5 requires that comments state what the code does.

**The sites, read here at `63f603f1a`, and the property each must state:**

| Site | Now reads | Must state |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `free-drag/spec.ts:223–226` | _the kernel's channel over the spec's `reportError`_, _two channels over one sink_, _neither channel encodes severity_ | The kernel's own route and this channel both reach `#deliver`: one route and one channel over one sink. Neither encodes severity |
| `free-drag/spec.ts:207` | _the kernel's channels refuse …_ | The kernel's own route refuses after applied destruction and discards a throwing handler |
| `sortable/spec.ts:1693` | _Its callers are the kernel's channels_ | Its callers are the kernel's own route, with the same two rules, and `panic`'s one delivery |
| `kernel/kernel.ts:413` | _the channels_, among the `readonly` fields | The controller's route |
| `kernel/unwind.ts:30` | _one controller's channel_ | The guard is built over one notifier: a minted channel or the controller's route |
| `kernel/unwind.ts:37` | _the channel's own guard_ | The notifier's terminus is what guarantees it does not throw |
| `kernel/spec.ts:98`, published | _each call allocates one closure_ | Each call allocates one function. The advice to mint one per sink at construction stays |

**The implementer may choose the words.** Only these sites change. Nothing else in those comments changes unless a listed sentence requires it. **This is not a census.** A further stale phrase met along the way is reported and left as it is.

**No separate recheck.** The commit's identity checks are mechanical (§29.6, step 1), and the final proof reads the text. Precedent for a delta recheck, §22's 5R.1, addressed a defect that the pass itself had introduced. Here the fix moves no code and comes with a fixed site list.

### 29.5 Attribution: what §24.4 requires of a control

§24.4 stands. This section states how it is read, so that an unexplained interval cannot pass as an explained one.

**A control is re-declared only if its whole interval is attributed:**

- **The interval** runs from the row's declared figure (2026-09-09) to its figure at the final tree.
- **Every step in it is a reading:** a before figure, an after figure and a named landed change, cited to a dated record.
- **The chain is continuous at each seam:** one step's after figure is the next step's before figure, at the same tree or across a change named as moving nothing. This is `budget-rebases.md`'s standard at the restoration.
- **The steps sum to the interval exactly,** with no line reading _rest_, _other_, _rounding_ or _toolchain_ without a reading behind it. Brotli does not add across changes, so these are measured endpoints and not decomposed costs.

**Where the records leave a seam open,** the implementer may run the existing `measureAll()` at that seam's named commits, in a detached worktree that is removed afterwards. It is deterministic, it is not a statistical round, and it needs no new harness. The worktree's dependencies are rebuilt at that commit, because a stale `box-quad` build reports a false zero (`budget-rebases.md`, 2026-08-31). A reading that cannot be reproduced leaves the seam open.

**A dependency update is a named change only with a reading on both sides of it.** `bfd2832db` and `01d88645f` touch dependency manifests inside the interval, as `git log` shows. Baseline B carries no drag2 code and still moved from 6,889 to 6,916 B, so a toolchain cause is a hypothesis for that row and is not established. Nothing here assumes it.

**A commit that a reading locates is attributed only if its change can reach that row:** through the row's module graph, or as a dependency or build change with the reading above. Otherwise the commit is reported, and the drift stays unexplained.

**An unattributed interval:**

- **Is not re-declared.** That control keeps its declared figure and stays red, by name.
- **Is stated explicitly** in `budget-rebases.md`: its last reading, the unexplained amount, and the seam where the chain breaks.
- **Is a finding for the architect,** as §24.4 and §24.6 state. It stops the sequence before `d208-requests.md`.

**Refused for any control:**

- moving its ceiling, or making it a ceiling;
- removing or suspending it;
- re-declaring only the attributed part of it;
- describing the drift as noise.

**Owner:** the implementer. **Input:** this section's commit. Do the steps in order, and a step that escalates stops the sequence. No retained-heap, allocation or timing round is owed (§28.7, §29.1). A source comment carries no record identifier.

**Step 1: the comment-only commit.** The sites of §29.3, in their own commit. Before committing, check and record:

- `just build`'s declarations differ from `c0713204a`'s only in `kernel/spec.d.ts`, only in that doc comment;
- the new `src/` tree hash.

If any of these fails, restore, stop and report.

- Read every row beside step 1's ceilings. No ceiling moves. `drag.js` stays at 142 and baseline B at its reading.
- State M-1's carry as §29.1 does.

**Written only when all of these hold:**

- §29.6's steps 1 to 3 are complete, and their checks are recorded;
- every control is re-declared, or its unattributed interval has been disposed of by the architect and is named in the record;
- no escalation or finding from §24.5 is open;

If one does not hold, the implementer stops without creating the file. Creating it early falsifies the witness.

### 29.8 What writing the record does

**Writing the file reddens `decisions.node`'s _should hold every witness it claims_, by design.** The row reports `D-208: .plan/measurements/d208-requests.md exists`. The test reads a false witness as a stale row, and only the whole-decision acceptance may remove that row (§20.5). So from the record's commit until acceptance:

- the failing set gains that row, by name. The record says so;
- the row is **not** deleted, the marker stays, and the witness is not weakened. D-208 is still not implemented;
- the branch therefore cannot read clean before acceptance. That is a correct gate on the merge.

### 29.9 After the record: the final proof and acceptance

**The final proof runs within the consolidator's review round,** as the owner has planned. This replaces _step 6's feature proof_ by a standalone reviewer in §20.5, §23.3, §24.5 step 6 and §24.6, as to where it runs and not what it owes:

- **It stays an independent feature proof** of the whole decision against this record, the contract and `d208-requests.md`, at the record's commit.
- **It does not reopen a3 or run new rounds to resolve it,** and it tests that the record states a3, its limits and the owner's words truthfully.
- **Findings go to the architect** through the consolidator. A source defect is not fixed by the round, and the freeze holds (§21.8).

**Then, in order:** the architect's whole-decision acceptance, which alone removes D-208's row and marker, closes F-432 to F-435 and F-441, and marks D-208 implemented. The merge comes after that and is the owner's.
